# Instrument-finding ledger

**Authority**: `TODO.md` protocol rule 8 (**D125**, ruled 2026-08-11). Append-only — a correction is a new entry naming the entry it corrects, never an edit.

**What belongs here**: any finding whose *subject is the verification apparatus itself* — checker/self-test defects, decision-document prose or arithmetic, stale locators, tracker-count drift, gate-instrument honesty. It takes **no task ID and no `TODO.md` row**.

**What does NOT belong here**: a *product* defect found *by* an instrument — the Q112 class (shipped code that fails to compile under a feature gate, a policy violated in code, a wrong byte on the wire). Those mint a row immediately, per protocol rule 2, exactly as before D125.

**The line to draw when unsure**: does fixing it change what a *user's* seal/verify does? Row. Does it change only what a *checker* says or how a document reads? Ledger.

**Promotion**: an entry becomes a task (protocol rule 2 — the registrar assigns the next free ID; lanes never self-number) only when it **blocks an acceptance on the ship path**, which is the Current-focus queue in `TODO.md`. Record the promotion as a new ledger entry pointing at the minted ID.

**Pre-D125 rows**: open instrument-class rows minted before 2026-08-11 stay in `TODO.md`, frozen as a class — they are NOT migrated here and NOT struck. This ledger starts empty and covers findings from wave 16 onward.

**Entry format** (one line each):

    - YYYY-MM-DD · <finder: lane / instrument / session> · <one-line finding> · <pointer: file / section / run id>

---

- 2026-08-12 · R20 lane · **`scripts/wasm-test-runner.mjs:119` cannot report which wasm32 test failed** — it does `Buffer.from(new Uint8Array(memory.buffer)).toString("latin1")` over the *whole* linear memory, which the real module grows from 2,949,120 B to **1,363,542,016 B** during `main()`, against node's max string of 536,870,888 B; so any failing `antseal-core` wasm32 test yields `Error: Cannot create a string longer than 0x1fffffe8 characters` instead of R41's promised `::error::wasm32 test runner: test '…' FAILED`. The lane still exits 1, so it is **red for the wrong reason and a reader learns nothing** — the "nonzero is not the message" failure mode exactly. **`--self-test` is structurally unable to see it**: its throwaway crate's memory is a few MiB, and it passed while the real lane was crashing. Fix measured at one function — 64 MiB windows with an 8 KiB overlap recovered the test name and the full panic record · scripts/wasm-test-runner.mjs:119 · found by using the runner as a witness, not by reading it

- 2026-08-12 · U28 lane · **a new form of the shared-build-lock hazard: `TaskStop` stops the task *wrapper*, not the compiler that task spawned.** A stopped lane's `cargo test` kept running and held the cargo build lock for **~15 minutes**, stalling the other lanes, until its PID was killed directly. The standing "parallel lanes share one working tree" rule covers `git` conflicts and multi-minute lock stalls, but it does not cover **orphaned children of a stopped task** — and that is the worst version of the case, because once the wrapper is gone there is no report to read, no exit to wait for and nothing that will ever announce the process, so the stall presents as an unexplained slowdown in lanes that did nothing wrong. The protocol gap is on the *stopping* side: a stop must be followed by a process check, never assumed to have taken the build with it · wave protocol · sibling of the "parallel lanes share one working tree" rule

- 2026-08-12 · D128-implementation lane · **`tests/doc_pointer_liveness.rs` recognises no pointer written with an ELIDED PREFIX, which makes an abbreviated citation unfalsifiable.** The checker worked as designed during this lane — it caught two renamed tests still cited by their old names, *"2 dangling doc pointer(s) — each reads as evidence and is not"* — but `normalise` requires `token.starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_lowercase())`, so a backtick span written `` `..._has_exactly_two_callers` `` (or with a leading `…`, or a leading `_`) is rejected as **not a pointer at all** rather than checked and found dangling, and passes in silence. The asymmetry is the sharp half: **trailing** elision is a first-class supported form — a `*` suffix sets `glob` and resolves by prefix — while **leading** elision is invisible, and leading is the natural way to shorten a long test name in prose. So an abbreviated citation reads as evidence and is never checked · crates/antseal-core/tests/doc_pointer_liveness.rs `normalise` · found while repairing the pointers the checker had just caught

- 2026-08-17 · orchestrator (wave-23 close), mechanism corrected by the wave-23 ledger lane · **`scripts/verifier-page-build.sh --check` reds exactly once after any commit that moves HEAD, and no reader of the red can tell which of the two stalenesses it is — by design, because the guard refuses the handle that would distinguish them.** The trigger is **not** the footer digest. `stale_guard()` rebuilds and compares the `sha256` of the **module** (`target/wasm-pack/antseal-wasm/antseal_wasm_bg.wasm`) before and after; what puts the commit inside those bytes is `crates/antseal-wasm/build.rs:24,40` emitting `cargo::rustc-env=ANTSEAL_SOURCE_COMMIT` (with `rerun-if-env-changed`) into `build_info.rs:26`'s `pub const SOURCE_COMMIT`, and `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh:226` defaulting that variable to `git rev-parse HEAD`. Verified without building: `strings` over the cached module returns the literal 40-hex `59c47ad9…afc15`, which **is** HEAD — so the stamp demonstrably lives in the module's data section, exactly where D129 §1 located it by offset and D135 §1.5 diagnosed the same event. The footer digest is a **consequence** the error message lists, not the trigger, and citing it sends a reader to the wrong file. **The ambiguity claim needs the same correction**: `local-gate.sh`'s `run()` tails 25 lines of a failed lane and `stale_guard` returns immediately, so `STALE ARTIFACT`, both digests and *"re-run to package the current module"* **do** reach the log. What a reader cannot tell is **which** staleness — a benign HEAD move that changed only the 40-byte stamp, or a real source change packaged from an old module — because the script's own comment **refuses arm (c)**, *"an mtime or commit-stamp predicate … neither handle sees an uncommitted edit"*. The discriminator exists but is not in the message: `git diff --name-only <prev>..HEAD -- crates/antseal-core/src crates/antseal-wasm Cargo.lock Cargo.toml .cargo rust-toolchain.toml` being **empty** is how D135 §1.5 settled it. Fixed by ANCHORING on `untrusted comment: ` (`scripts/ci-lanes.sh:1145`): a secret key's comment BEGINS with the tool name, a signature's begins with `signature from`. An exclusion was refused on measurement — `ex()`'s `--exclude` flags apply to all seven rules at once, so `--exclude='*.minisig'` would blind the whole lane and make a real key renamed `foo.minisig` invisible; a fixture now reds BY NAME if a future lane tries that shortcut. Rule (4b) was kept, not removed: over real keys of every form D71 cares about its true positives are a subset of (4c)'s **but for one case** — a key whose body is absent and whose header survives. **Ledger and not a row** under rule 8: nothing a user seals, verifies or restores changes. - 2026-08-27 · orchestrator (wave 31) · **A wrapper's exit status reported the opposite of the gate's, and only the recorded value was true.** The gate was invoked as `./scripts/local-gate.sh > log 2>&1; echo "GATE_REAL_EXIT=$?" | tee file`; the harness reported the command as **exit code 0**, because the shell's status came from `tee`. The value inside the file was **1**, and the gate was genuinely red with `verifier-page` FAIL. **A pipeline's status is its LAST element's**, so any `… | tee` wrapper silently discards the status it was written to preserve. This register already refuses to read `cargo`'s 101 as a test failure and refuses `--plan`'s green fields as a run; this is the same class one layer out — **read the recorded `REAL_EXIT`, never the wrapper's**. `set -o pipefail` or `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` is the fix where a pipe is wanted at all · `scripts/local-gate.sh` invocation convention
- 2026-08-27 · orchestrator (wave 31, post-registration) · **A line-level exclusion in a redaction sweep hid two real hits on the line it excluded, and the sweep reported CLEAN.** Verifying a personal-identifier redaction, the check was `grep -rnI -E '<patterns>' . | grep -v 'substituta'` — the exclusion existing because `substitutable` contains the mail provider's name as a substring. `grep -v` filters **LINES**, and this register's lines run to thousands of characters: one benign `substitutable` in a `tasks/Q.md` note suppressed **six** real occurrences on that same line, including the account name the redaction existed to remove. The sweep printed nothing and was believed. Caught only because an independent walk of the historical blobs enumerated string forms the worktree sweep had said were absent. **The propagating rule: an exclusion must operate at the same granularity as the thing it excludes.** A match-level sweep (`grep -o` over the pattern union, then count by form) cannot hide a same-line hit, and is what every later verification here used. This is the *assertions-that-cannot-fail* class wearing a new coat: the predicate could not report the defect it was written to find · redaction verification, `tasks/Q.md`

- 2026-08-28 · orchestrator (wave 31 follow-up) · **A force-push removed the identifiers from the branch and left a working index to them in plain sight, and `clone --mirror` reported CLEAN throughout.** After `+ c2709ea...68d7a88 main -> main (forced update)`, a fresh `git clone --mirror` of the remote scanned to **0** occurrences — the obvious predicate, and the wrong one. A public reader would not have to guess a 40-hex value; the repository hands over the pointer and the pointer resolves. **The propagating rule: when a removal's success predicate is *"the thing I can reach is clean"*, verify by the route an adversary would take, not the route the tool offers.** The closing predicate here is a **404** from a direct-SHA fetch, obtainable only from GitHub Support's garbage collection · `Q268`
- 2026-08-29 · orchestrator (wave 32) · **`cargo update --dry-run` UNDER-REPORTS, and a whole row's refusal had been priced on it.** `Q267`'s recorded refusal said the minimal advisory fix "also moves" a set of Windows-target crates. Two lessons, opposite directions: *an "is this minimal?" judgement resting on `--dry-run` is an assertion that cannot fail*, and *a diff line count is not a graph change*. The fix that landed touches **two** lines. Pointer: `Q267`'s tick note.

The wave's lean — write the missing artifacts — would have re-created work already in the tree. A negative result from a non-recursive listing is not evidence about a tree. Pointer: `D166` §0, `Q31`'s wave-32 note.

All 24 `actions/checkout` steps in the 8 workflows run at the default `fetch-depth: 1`, so wiring the scanner into CI as it stood would have produced a permanent, cheerful, meaningless green. **An invariant that balances over a truncated corpus is not an invariant over the corpus you meant.** Fixed under `D165` §2 R6 by refusing a shallow store outright — keyed on shallowness, never on `commit=1`, which is legitimate for a new repository. Pointer: `Q266`'s tick note.

The cause was not a stale artifact: under **default features the crate is not in the graph at all** (`cargo tree -e normal -i <crate> --workspace` → *package ID specification did not match any packages*). The honest run needed `--all-features`, which did compile it and stamped a **new** fingerprint. **A build that succeeds is evidence about the graph it built, not about the change you made** — and the two can be disjoint. Discriminator, cheap and worth making routine: grep the log for the `Compiling <crate>` line and check the fingerprint mtime before believing a build verified a dependency change. Pointer: `Q267`'s tick note.

Caught only because the size was implausible and was checked against the estimate instead of accepted. Rebuilt with `--exclude='*/target'`: **26.5 MB, 2 539 members, zero `/target/` members**. This is the wave-31 ruling restated for a third venue — *an exclusion must operate at the same granularity as the thing it excludes* — and the twist worth keeping is that **the estimate and the archive used different tools whose flags look identical and are not**. The archive was then verified by **extraction and recursive `diff -r` against the live tree** (`DIFF_REAL_EXIT=0`, zero differences), not by trusting the write, because a `cmp` straight after a copy can be answered from the page cache.

The authentic bytes were in the CI artifact **and** un-tracked on the maintainer's own disk the whole time; measured this wave they yield **the same error code, the same 5120 B peak and the same 10112 B total** the record attributes to its corrupt transcription. So the metadata was right, only the prose base64 was damaged, and **the damage was behaviourally inert** — the record's substantive ruling is untouched, which is why this is an addendum and not a retraction. **The rule: *and never will* is a claim about the world's evidence stores rather than about the record, and it is the one claim a record must not make over an unsearched corpus — CI artifacts, local scratch and backups are all enumerable, and an un-tracked file is not a lost one.** · `docs/decisions/D102-parser-structural-allocation-cost.md` §7
- 2026-09-12 · fuzz-verification lane (wave 34) · **A register under-counted its own evidence by half, and the uncited half sharpens the inference the counted half produced.** `A100` and `D102` §7 both reason from *"four"* `anchor_ots` CI reds; there are **eight** `fuzz-smoke-artifacts`, and the sizes **389, 823, 840 and 915 B** appear nowhere in `TODO.md` or `docs/` as artifact sizes — the tree's sole `840` is `tasks/A.md:1167`'s `4 840 B` relative cap, a neighbouring number rather than this one, which is exactly how a count like this stays wrong through two records. The four uncited runs also test `A100`'s own inference, that a second input *"peaked at the same 5120 B — evidence the peak is a fixed step, not input-proportional"*: the **840 B** input peaks at **10240 B**, the next rung of the doubling ladder `D102` §1.1 prints at `:69` (`160 320 640 1280 2560 5120 10240 …`). The peak is therefore a fixed **LADDER**, not a fixed step — a **sharpening, not a refutation**, and the D58-versus-relative-cap disagreement the record rules on is unaffected. **The rule: a count of reds is a count of ARTIFACTS and is re-measured from the artifact store rather than carried forward in prose; and an inference about a quantity that can only double, drawn from two observations, is under-determined by construction.** · `TODO.md` `A100`; `docs/decisions/D102-parser-structural-allocation-cost.md` §1.1, §7
- 2026-09-12 · registrar (wave 34) · **Recording the finding above TRIPPED the arm it describes: the guard's prose rule read this ledger's own sentence as naming an account, and the token it flagged was the new arm's own name.** `NAMEPROSE` is `\b(?:user|account|login)[ \t]?names?\b[^\n]{0,72}?` followed by a QUOTED `IDENT_NAME`, so an entry that must state in prose what `REGISTERED_HOME_NAMES`' keys are, and then name the arm in backticks a few words later, emits `[USER-NEW] … (1 occurrence(s), digest 54ee7225d5bac4ca)` with `SELF-LITERAL` as the supposed account. This is the **SECOND** measured instance of the class the rule's own comment already records — the first, `match-level` at `tasks/Q.md:3492`, is why `**bold**` is not admitted as a quote — and it was repaired by DE-ANCHORING the ledger prose, never by widening the rule. **How it was attributed is half the entry**: the tree was concurrently red from another lane's in-flight edits, and `[USER-NEW]`/`[HOME-CEILING]` are AGGREGATE findings that name **no path**, so *"no finding names my file"* (`grep -c` = 0) was not evidence of innocence — the per-file answer, obtained by importing the module and calling its pure `identity_names()` on the added lines alone, **inverted** it. **Three rules: a guard whose anchors are ordinary English is tripped by its own documentation, so every surface that describes it must be written around those anchors; an aggregate finding cannot be attributed by grep, and a checker with a pure per-text function can always be asked the per-file question directly instead; and the entry recording a guard is itself scanned by that guard.** · `scripts/check-personal-data.py` `NAMEPROSE`; this wave's `SELF-LITERAL` entry
- 2026-09-12 · orchestrator (wave 34) · **The pre-public wave's OWN registration reproduced all three identifier classes it was registering, and the guard extended earlier in the same wave caught every one — at occurrence 132 of an accepted 131.** Writing the wave-34 Current-focus block into `TODO.md` emitted `PD_REAL_EXIT=1` with three findings: `[DENY/handle]` (the personal address's username stem, written out while recording that it measures zero), `[HOME-CEILING]` (*"a registered home directory name grew to **132** occurrence(s) against an accepted ceiling of 131"* — the registration added the 132nd while explaining why the 131 are accepted), and `[USER-NEW]`, where the prose arm extracted the English article **the** from the phrase *"names the username in prose"* and treated it as though it were an account. That was the **third** over-broad match measured in this checker in one wave: the other two were a rule that read its own docstring, and a rule that sat behind an anchorless prefilter and so could never fire at all. **A fourth arrived while this very entry was being written** — the arm matched its own subject again, extracting a rule's backticked name from a sentence that had to say what the arm does, which makes the false-positive class **structural**: this rule cannot be documented in prose without firing, so every record about it must be de-anchored by hand. Four instances, one wave, all in documents describing the rule; a narrowing (POSIX account names are conventionally lower-case, and English articles are not accounts) is **owed as a follow-up and was deliberately NOT taken here**, because widening or narrowing a guard in the hours before an irreversible publication is the one edit whose blast radius nobody can measure in time. **This is `Q268`'s exact defect class — a document about an identifier reproducing that identifier — recurring in the tracker rather than in a scrub report, in the one wave whose purpose was to prevent it, and authored by the orchestrator who had just briefed two lanes on the rule.** The transferable lesson is not *be careful*: it is that **the only reason this was caught is that the bare-name arm and the ceiling had landed hours earlier**, so the instrument built this wave immediately convicted its own author. Repaired by naming every class and reproducing no literal — the rule `Q269` states — never by widening a rule or raising a ceiling on the eve of an irreversible publication. **Raising the ceiling to 132 would have been the tempting fix and it is the wrong one**: the ceiling's whole function is that accepted is not a licence to add more, and the first thing that asked to be exempted from it was the wave's own prose.
- 2026-09-12 · orchestrator (wave 34) · **HEAD IS A WASM INPUT, so the gate can never be green on the commit that is about to be made — and the register's recorded reason for three waves of `STALE ARTIFACT` was wrong in its conclusion while right in its verdict.** `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh:226` reads `commit="${ANTSEAL_SOURCE_COMMIT:-$(git rev-parse HEAD 2>/dev/null || printf 'unknown')}"` and `crates/antseal-wasm/build.rs` stamps it into the module, with `cargo::rerun-if-env-changed` making the stamp non-stale. Measured on the artifact the gate had just built: the current commit is present (**1**), the immediately previous commit **absent (0)**, and a fabricated 40-hex control **absent (0)** so the search was live — the first control tried, forty ASCII zeros, matched the binary's data section and had to be discarded as unable to fail. **The consequence is procedural and it inverts the wave-close order**: every commit invalidates the packaged module, so a gate run immediately after a commit rebuilds and FAILS on that rebuild, and only a second run over an unmoved `HEAD` can pass. The standing rule *"a wasm-input change means the gate needs TWO runs"* is therefore the weaker form of *"a COMMIT means two runs, and every wave commits"* — which is why waves 32, 33 and 34 each needed an extra run and each explained it differently. **The wave-33 register's stated ground — that the check's commit-stamp arm being refused makes the module "a pure function of the source tree" — is FALSIFIED**: the refused arm is the *checker's*, and it says nothing about the *builder*, which supplies the commit by default. **A diff-based wasm-input test cannot see this input at all**, because the input is not a file; this orchestrator ran exactly that test three times, twice reached the right answer for the wrong reason, and was corrected only when a third run reddened on a tree whose diff contained no Rust, no lockfile and no template.
- 2026-09-12 · orchestrator (wave 34) · **COMMITTING BEFORE GATING SILENTLY DISARMS THE HEAVY-FEATURES AND DEVNET-E2E TRIGGERS, and the two wave-34 findings about the gate pull in OPPOSITE directions.** `scripts/gate-features.sh:187` defaults its base to **local `main`** (`base="${ANTSEAL_GATE_BASE:-main}"`) and line 194 unions `git diff --name-only "$base"...HEAD` with `git status --porcelain`. On `main` with a clean tree **both terms are empty**, so `heavy-features n/a (no storage-touching path changed vs main)` is **guaranteed rather than measured** — the dominant defect class of this project, sitting inside the gate's own trigger. Measured at the wave-34 close: default base **0 files**, `origin/main...HEAD` **9 files**; re-derived against the published base the verdict *holds* (`no storage-touching path changed vs origin/main`, `REAL_EXIT=1`), so this wave's `n/a` and its `e2e-devnet SKIP` are both honest — **but they were about to be right for a reason that would have held no matter what the wave contained.** The interaction is the entry: **`HEAD` being a wasm input pushes the gate AFTER the commit, while the trigger base being local `main` requires it BEFORE** — so **no single run on `main` satisfies both**, and a wave that gates only post-commit gets an unfalsifiable heavy verdict while a wave that gates only pre-commit gets a `verifier-page` red it will misattribute. **Remedy, and it is one environment variable: close the wave with `ANTSEAL_GATE_BASE=origin/main`** — the right base for the question a wave close actually asks, *what does this wave change relative to what is published* — after rebuilding the module for the final commit. Both halves then measure something that can fail.

- 2026-09-12 · orchestrator (wave 34, at the flip) · **A GitHub settings write returned HTTP 200 with a full repository object and silently changed nothing — twice — and only the mandated read-back caught it.** Enabling `security_and_analysis.secret_scanning_non_provider_patterns` via `PATCH /repos/{o}/{r}` answered **200**, `REAL_EXIT=0`, and echoed a complete repo body; the field read back **`disabled`** immediately after, and again after a retry that named the parent `secret_scanning` in the same payload. The setting requires Advanced Security, which this account does not have — **and the API says so nowhere**: no 403, no 422, no error field, no warning. **The transferable rule is the one the publish runbook already insists on and this is its first measured vindication at a real flip: every settings write is followed by a read of THE FIELD IT CLAIMS TO SET, and a 200 plus a plausible response body is not evidence that anything changed.** Recorded as OFF rather than reported as enabled. Its sibling `secret_scanning_validity_checks` was **deliberately refused** on a different ground — it transmits detected secret candidates to third-party providers to test liveness, which is an **egress** decision outside anything consented to, and is not the kind of switch to flip silently while closing a flip.
- 2026-09-12 · orchestrator (wave 34, at the flip) · **A hardening read-back that was load-bearing BEFORE the flip becomes a 422 AFTER it, so the runbook's own verification step stops being answerable at the moment it matters.** `actions/permissions/access` read `{"access_level":"none"}` on the private repo and B5b instructs the operator to confirm the three hardening settings *"have not moved"*. So the pre-flip value was never a thing the flip could preserve, and an operator following B5b literally would read the 422 as a regression or as a failed check. **Lesson: a verification step whose endpoint's applicability depends on the very state being changed must say what its answer looks like on BOTH sides of the change** — otherwise it is a check that cannot pass exactly when it is needed. The two hardening settings that *do* survive were confirmed: `default_workflow_permissions: read`, `can_approve_pull_request_reviews: false`.

- 2026-10-15 · orchestrator (consented acts) · **A hosted job log can hide its own verdict line from `grep`.** GitHub keeps cargo-deny's ANSI colour codes INSIDE the line, so `grep -E 'advisories (ok|FAILED)'` over `ci` run `38788439680`'s `audit-deny` job log returned nothing for a GREEN job; with `sed -E 's/\x1b\[[0-9;]*m//g'` applied first, the same file reads `advisories ok, bans ok, licenses ok, sources ok`. The dangerous direction is the converse: a grep for a red pattern such as `error[vulnerability]` can miss a coloured red line and read as clean. Every hosted-log quotation in the consented-acts record was taken from the stripped text, and *no verdict line found* is recorded as unknown, never as a verdict.

- 2026-10-16 · orchestrator (wave 37), on lane L-CLI's measurement · **A shared `target/` is safe for parallel builds and unsafe for parallel spawned-binary tests across feature sets.** Cargo releases the build-directory lock before it runs the tests it built. So another lane's `cargo test -p antseal-cli --features ant-backend` re-linked the shared `target/debug/antseal` at 00:54:34Z (478 MB; it contains *"connecting the read-only reader"*, a string compiled only into the `ant-backend` module) while lane L-CLI's default-build run was still executing. Every spawned-binary test of that run after that moment ran the feature binary under default-build expectations. Two went red for no reason in the code under test: `upgrade_hook::every_subcommand_either_arms_the_hook_or_is_a_documented_non_armer`, and `verify_command::live_without_a_compiled_backend_refuses_at_the_seam` (exit 43 where 23 was expected). The swap can as easily turn a red green, so the whole run is void, passes included. Remedy taken: a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` per lane for any run of spawned-binary tests, and plants applied in a private tree copy. A plant applied in the shared tree can be compiled into another lane's build. · `docs/decisions/D180-a-configured-rpc-url-is-refused-at-load-and-the-arm-sentence-no-build-prints-is-deleted.md` §6

- 2026-10-17 · orchestrator (consented push) · **The hosted yank check cannot see a yank made after its restored cache was saved.** At `5050f5b` the `ci` `audit-deny` job printed `warning[yanked-not-detected]: yanked crate was not encountered` for `deny.toml`'s `zip@2.4.2` entry, while the crates.io API still reads `yanked: true` for 2.4.2 and the same lane run locally on the same tree detects the yank. cargo-deny 0.19.8 took yank state from cargo's local index cache and did not refresh it: a full local run left the cached `zip` index entry at its 2026-10-16 04:17Z mtime, still without `zip 9.0.4` (published 09:09Z that day) and still listing the since-unyanked 2.6.1 as yanked. Both hosted venues, `ci.yml`'s `audit-deny` job and `advisory-cron.yml`, restore a `Swatinem/rust-cache` cache (this run by partial key), so their index is as old as the cache. D184 §2 R3's *"an upstream yank of any locked version … reds the required `audit-deny` context … and the weekly `advisory-cron`"* therefore holds locally and on a fresh-cache runner, and is blind on a restored one to every yank newer than its cache — an assertion that cannot fail in exactly the venue meant to catch a fresh yank. Recorded, not wired: the fix (refresh the graph's index entries before the check, or keep the registry index out of these jobs' caches) changes two workflows and goes to the next planning round. · `scripts/ci-lanes.sh` (`lane_audit_deny`); `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (`audit-deny`); `.github/workflows/advisory-cron.yml`; `deny.toml`; `tasks/Q.md` `Q280`; `docs/decisions/D184-the-yanked-chacha20-and-wnaf-move-now-and-a-yank-reds-the-advisory-lane.md` (addendum 2026-10-17)

- 2026-10-17 · orchestrator (wave 38) · **The shell's `grep` and `rg` were functions routed to a broken binary, and every lane needed telling.** In this session's shell snapshot both names resolved to a wrapper that printed *"claude native binary not installed"* and matched nothing, so a bare `grep` returned an empty result that reads exactly like a clean search. `command grep` bypasses the function; child scripts were unaffected. Lane L-NET hit a second form of the same trap: `git ls-files -z | xargs -0 command grep …` printed nothing, because xargs cannot run the `command` builtin and `2>/dev/null` hid the error. A zero-hit grep is evidence only when the same invocation has found something first. · the session's shell environment (not a repository file)
- 2026-10-17 · wave-38 lane L-ANCHOR, fixed by the orchestrator · **A readers–writer `flock` starves its writer, so no lane could plant a fault in the shared tree.** Linux `flock` grants a new shared request while an exclusive one waits. L-NET's cold private-target `ant-backend` build held the shared lock for about two hours, and the other lanes' runs overlapped it. Two exclusive requests (540 s and 900 s) timed out unstarted, and one waited 52 min (23:47 to 00:38 local); for most of round 1, `plants.log` held only shadow-copy plants. L-VOCAB measured the same starvation (`flock -x -w 900` timed out unstarted), and noted that one lane's suite script held one shared lock across all its cargo runs. The fix was a writer turnstile: a reader passes `flock -s turnstile.lock true` at its outermost level, and a writer holds the turnstile `-x` through its wait and its plant. A shadow copy with a private target directory needs no lock. Once it was in place, the starved plant acquired as soon as the last earlier reader finished, and the next writer acquired 6 s after the turnstile was released. · the wave's lock protocol (scratch, not committed); lane report L-ANCHOR §4 and the wave-38 plan log (scratch)

Lanes are safe only by the convention of appending. · `docs/threat-model.md`; `docs/reviews/codeql-triage-2026-09-13.md`; `scripts/check-traceability.py` (`doc-links`)

- 2026-10-17 · wave-38 lane L-ERR1 · **Killing a `flock` wrapper does not release its lock.** `flock(1)` passes the locked descriptor to the command it runs. When a lane stopped its own suite chain to unblock its plant, the orphaned `cargo test` kept the shared lock until cargo and its child were killed too, which a scan of `/proc/*/fd` confirmed. A "stop my reader" step has to kill the process tree and then check `/proc/*/fd`. Lane L-NET measured the same from the other side: the linker itself held the descriptor (`/proc/<lld>/fd/3` → `tree.lock`), so neither killing `flock` nor `flock -o` shortens a hold. A private-target build should run in a copy of the tree from the start, taking the tree lock only for the copy. · the wave's lock protocol (scratch, not committed)

- 2026-10-19 · orchestrator (wave-39 acts, read-only `gh api` and the two analyses' SARIF) · **A CodeQL alert is keyed by the text from its line onward, so an edit just after a dismissed alert re-opens it under a new number, and a move alone does not.** The analysis of `4aa04b6` closed `#8` (`rust/cleartext-logging`, `crates/antseal-cli/src/vault/keyfile.rs:437`, dismissed *used in tests*) as `fixed` and opened `#67` on the same code at `:441`. The cause first recorded, in `TODO.md`'s 2026-10-19 evening acts paragraph (the 4-line move), was wrong. An alert's identity is its `primaryLocationLineHash`, a rolling hash of the 100 non-blank characters from the start of the flagged line, and a local re-implementation reproduces all six hashes that the analyses of `07a170c` (`1979395115`) and `4aa04b6` (`1983498613`) recorded for `vault/keyfile.rs` and `vault/cipher.rs`. `#25` (`cipher.rs:322` to `:325`) and `#26` (`keyfile.rs:261` to `:265`) moved and kept their hashes, numbers and dismissals. `#8`'s line sat three lines from the end of the file, so its window ran through the end of its test, and `U104` inserted new test code there (`ddd3b4f7afe2ec16:1` to `601112c5cb0bf0fb:1`). Two controls: the `4aa04b6` file with that test removed hashes `:441` to `#8`'s own value, and the `07a170c` file with four lines inserted above `:261` keeps both hashes. So any edit inside that window of a dismissed alert re-opens it as a new open alert, which needs its own consented dismissal.

- 2026-10-20 · wave-40 orchestrator (a shell hazard in writing briefs) · **An unquoted heredoc ran a backticked file name in a lane brief as a command and silently dropped it from the sentence.** Written through an unquoted `<<EOF`, the AS-BUILT-2 brief's `` `init.rs` `` became a command substitution that failed and left an empty string, so the write succeeded with a sentence missing its file name; a grep for the damaged sentence caught it before dispatch, and the brief was repaired. - 2026-10-20 · wave-41 lane L-NET4 (D208), on planning lane P1 · **P1's S51 plan could not be built as written, and its probe could not have shown it.** P1 §2(e) said *"Cargo: no manifest change"* while its R2, ruled as D208 R2, bounded the broadcast with `tokio::time::timeout`; antseal-net's library takes no tokio edge by recorded decision (S6; tokio is a dev-dependency only, per the root and `crates/antseal-net/Cargo.toml` comments). - 2026-10-20 · wave-41 lane L-NET4 (D208), on the orchestrator's asks · **Two premises in the orchestrator's asks to L-NET4 were false: the runtime the CLI pays on, and a deadline alloy does not give.** The ask named *"the current-thread runtime the CLI uses"*; the payer's runtime is `new_multi_thread().enable_all()` (`crates/antseal-cli/src/backend.rs`, `runtime()`, D90 §3.3), a load-bearing choice pinned by `anchor_blocking_calls_require_a_multi_thread_runtime` and `a_spawned_task_progresses_while_the_runtime_thread_blocks`, and the lane measured both flavours anyway (the hung-gateway row G6 ended at 3.015 s multi-thread and 3.032 s current-thread). The ask also preferred alloy's `with_timeout` for receipt awaits, but in alloy-provider 1.8.3 `PendingTransactionBuilder::with_timeout` bounds `get_receipt` only while every lookup answers (probe: still waiting at 6 s for a 600 ms timeout when one lookup hangs; an error after 0.87 ms when one is dropped), so the lane wrote its own lookup loop with each lookup bounded. Any later use of `with_timeout` as a deadline in this repository is unsafe. · D208 §6; lane report L-NET4 §4 (2), (3)
- 2026-10-20 · wave-41 lane L-NET4 (D208 R13, R14) · **A first-draft "names no URL" test passed vacuously, because the deadline's message, not the redacted error, was the reason it read.** evmlib retries the ERC-20 balance and allowance reads three times, 4, 16 and 36 s apart (about 56 s), so under the draft's 300 ms deadline the reason was *"… no answer within 300 ms"*, which holds no URL whether or not the error path redacts; timing every row alone exposed it. The shipped row, `s57_the_retried_erc20_reads_name_no_url`, waits out the retries under a 90 s limit and asserts the positive needle *"error sending request for url (<url redacted>)"* beside the absences. - 2026-10-20 · wave-41 records lane AS-BUILT-1 · **The briefs' rule to write "`X` owns them" is style that no instrument checks in plain prose, and the first control built on it could not fail.** As the 2026-10-19 wave-39 L-DOCS entry recorded, `OWNED_BY_RE` runs only on lines that carry a POSIX ownership marker (`scripts/check-personal-data.py:420-425`), so a sentence planted in a copy of D69 in a throwaway repository, naming a made-up owner in backticks in the passive *owned-by* form the briefs forbid, left the guard at exit 0. - 2026-10-20 · wave-41 orchestrator (a shell hazard) · **The backtick hazard recurred in the orchestrator's own shell, this time through double quotes.** A double-quoted `echo` whose text carried `` `X` `` ran `X`, the X server's wrapper, which refused (*"Only console users are allowed to run the X server"*), so no harm followed; the 2026-10-20 wave-40 entry recorded the same hazard through an unquoted heredoc.
